# What the owner has asked for, by area

Standing rulings, latest words first where they changed. Read the area before changing it, then check the code: a point here
was either built or is still open. Add a ruling here the same day it is given and delete what it overrules (git keeps it; nothing is struck through). Quotes are the
owner's, typos fixed.

## Score editor
- **R74, the version and Edit two controls, side by side (owner, 2026-11-26: "i dont like this edit button with this accompaniment: dont you see how awful it look inside this control"; then "but why did you move it to the right: you always should ask you why i do this and does this comply to the examples"):** the capsule that held both is gone. The version (Accompaniment ▾, Your arrangement ▾) is its own glass menu button beside the song's name, as wide as its longest name; Edit is its own glass button right beside it (a pen on a phone, pen and Edit on a laptop), as the owner's Flat screenshots show "✎ Edit ▾" in the title's group after the transport; pressed on the sand wash while editing, in the same place in Play and Edit (the flow keeps the bar's frame on both sizes). Moving Edit to the bar's far end, on Apple's rule, was undone: the owner's example wins.
- **R74, no Done in the bar; the phone editor holds the laptop's model (owner, 2026-11-26: "and what is with this done button. why is it here"; "we are now on the player and editor not finished"):** the capsule's end is Edit in both modes, pressed on the sand wash while editing and a tap back to playing, as Flat's; the ink Done, which read as a save the app already makes and outshone the music, is gone on every board. The phone's ··· menu is the laptop title menu's rows (Credits, Transpose, Recordings; Export, Print) with the rail's places between them (Instruments, Layout, Audio), each a sheet with the laptop panel's own body, controls at 44; its dead rows (Note input, Recording, MIDI keyboard) and the old Layout page and Instruments card are gone.
- **R74, the laptop editor as Flat's new editor (owner, 2026-11-26: "this now are the new settings … we dont need legacy settings"; "the header look like this and the title like this with these options"):** a rail (Notation, Instruments, Layout, Audio; Search, Shortcuts) beside one panel; the song's name with ▾ is the score's menu (Credits, Transpose, Recordings, Export ›, Print, Keyboard shortcuts); the Version and mode capsule sits beside the name, never centred ("I dont like how this arrangement is centered"); Layout shows "Preview of your score"; the legacy Document settings page is gone. "Takes" is "Recordings" everywhere ("make proper wordings not takes").
- **R74, Credits a page and the title in the bar (owner, 2026-11-26: "like the title in the header and clickin on its edit button with pen icon we go to the credits page"; "it should open properly on a proper page … be carefull when you use teh popups"):** the laptop editor's bar carries the song's name and a pen as Flat's; Credits opens as a page of its own (Back, Credits, the fields and Save in one centred column) on both sizes, never a popover, sheet or window ("why the credits are still a popup and not a proper page like i said?"); the Page view fits its width beside the palette.
- **R74, the Player prints a real page (owner, 2026-11-26: "this accompaniment sheet doesnt look like a professioanal score"; "on the phone the accompaniment doesnt look like a real sheet score"):** the editor's A4 page with Dorico's spacing on a laptop, a page of the phone's width on the desk on a phone (three bars a line), the accompaniment credited in italic under the music's credit.
- **R74, a real toolbar, panes that resize, Page and Panorama only (owner, 2026-11-26: "reserach the flat editor workflow … we need real toolbar like on the laptop, real resizable interactions … remove the vertical scroll and leave only the page and panorama view and make proper layout for the sheet"):** Flat's 56 toolbar under the bar (the note-input pill at its start; Delete, Copy, Cut, Paste, zoom, Page · Panorama and the keys at its end; on a phone the same row scrolling sideways), the palette a panel at the left dragged from 280 to 520 and hidden by its switch, the keys dragged from 96 to 260; Page lays the song on A4 (17.2 mm margins, 7 mm staff, Words left and Music right, systems justified), Panorama one system at its natural spacing; the vertical view is gone; note spacing as Dorico's (3½ spaces a quarter, √2 per doubled value, 1½ at least). Replaces the palette docked at the right. Spec: `.scratch/r39/plan/score-editor.md`, R74.
- **The editor's gates as the EditorDraft boards (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** opening, offline and failed stood at the top of a bare screen with no way out but their one button (Try again left an editor with no Close at all); they stand centred under the editor's own glass Close, as the board's gates. A song that is not there says so (This song can’t be found, Back to Songs), where it said the connection had failed. The board's gate Close was a fill circle unlike the editor bar's; it is the bar's glass Close.
- **A laptop's card heads as the kit's POPOVER (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20; EditorLaptop, the run boards' Setup):** a sheet that is a card on a laptop (the editor's ···, the Player's gear, a run's Setup) kept the phone sheet's head, its title centred with Done or Close at its right; the boards' POPOVER sets the title at the start, 16 in, and has no Done, a click outside or Esc closing it. Every laptop card heads that way now; a page under the first keeps its Back and centred title, as POPOVER's `back`.
- **The editor's ··· as its boards, measured (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** its rows wore paint tiles (a sand H, a sky eye, a lilac arrow…) the boards never draw; they are plain rows now, each opening a page or sheet with its chevron (Takes, Export…, Shortcuts, Versions, Publish… had none), Print an act without one. An editor's draft says where it stands in the board's line under the menu's title (Published · no changes, Saved · not published, Saving…, crimson when not saved), and beside Close on a laptop, where it had a cloud beside the title on a phone and nothing on a laptop; its Versions is the first group's last row, Publish… and Discard changes the last group. The boards in turn drew rows that open nothing (On-screen piano, which has no page; a learner's Version history, which the app keeps none of), Instruments as Piano over a Melody and a Piano (the app counts 2), Export for a built-in draft (it writes the learner's own song), Print with a chevron, and a Publish in Explore card, a second way to the song Settings' Publish: all of it is gone, and the draft's sheet says Versions of Amazing Grace and Newest, as the app.
- **The score's line labels as the boards (the board-to-app pairing, 2026-11-20):** a line's label ("1 · Verse · ♩ = 80") shrank with the staff to 8.6 px on a phone, under the 12 floor, and wrote "♩= 80"; it is never under 12 now (14 on a laptop, as EditorLaptop's), with a space each side of "=". The chord names over the staff are bold in the book serif, as the Player boards draw them (the app's were semibold, the editor boards' a sans), one look in Play and Edit.
- **The Editor boards draw the song's real staves (the board-to-app pairing, 2026-11-20):** the four editor boards engraved Amazing Grace as a melody alone, while the song holds Voice and Piano (their own Instruments sheet says so) and the app engraves Melody over the Piano's grand staff; each system is now Melody with its chords and words, then the Piano's treble and bass under a brace, the r2 accompaniment pitch for pitch as the app prints it (the bass on the beat, the chord on two and three, G⁷'s F♮ once a bar), names on the first system, a bar number on the rest; two bars a system on a phone, four on a laptop, whose column widens to 1000 to use the room. The key signature's sharp now sits on F (it stood on D), on the song boards' covers too, and the heading says "Verse", as the song has one.
- **Recent's keys in their 44 cells (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** on a laptop the docked palette's Recent row drew 48-wide keys 44 apart, each over the next (the harness's OVERLAP, seven times); its keys sit inside their categories' wrappers, out of reach of the grid's `min-w-0` for its direct children. Every key in a grid now narrows to its cell, as the EditorLaptop board's row.
- **The palette's marks in the boards' own serif (type anatomy 7, 2026-11-18):** dynamics, ornaments and articulations (mf, p, tr, >) were set in Instrument Serif, a third face beside Iowan Old Style and Instrument Sans; they keep their engraved bold italic in Iowan now.

- **Loop round 61, Record a take in the note band (the Editor boards, loop round 46):** it was a row in the ··· menu; it stands between Piano keys and Play, a crimson dot in its ring, on both sizes, and is gone from the menu; a lesson's example has none (2026-11-17).

- **Loop round 61, no "Use for new songs" in Layout (research F22, not built):** a saved layout default would make Reset mean two things and add a hidden setting; every new song starts from the one layout, and Reset returns to it ("fewer settings") (2026-11-17).

- **Loop round 61, a slower hand's chord written as one (research F24):** keys struck within 80 ms were one chord, so a slower player's chord split into notes; the window is Dorico's default 100 ms, fixed, with no new setting (fewer settings) (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Shortcuts from the editor's menu on a laptop (research F23, Flat's rail; canvas v369):** the shortcuts sheet opened only with ?; where keys type them, the menu's settings end with Shortcuts, ? its value, on EditorLaptop and EditorDraftLaptop and in the app (2026-11-17).

- **Loop round 61, where the song is kept, under the menu's title (the boards' ED_SAVED):** the learner's own song or version said nowhere that it was saved; the menu says "Saved on this device", or "Saved to your account" when signed in, under the song's name (2026-11-17).

- **The page view as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "also the page view"):** the pages' canvas reaches the screen's edges with no rounded frame round it; each page's number stands at its head's outer corner, right on an odd page and left on an even one, as Flat and an engraver print it (the Player's pages too).
- **The palette as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "why don't you make the design exactly like the flat editor"; `flat-score-editor` 006–041, `palette-…` and the owner's 17:30 shots):** folded, the chosen category's tools in one row of bare glyphs in soft ink, the chosen on a sand wash; under them, at the sheet's foot, the categories as Flat's tab bar of signs (♩, ♭♯, >, tr, ppp, the barline, 𝄞, ♯♯, 4/4, the repeat, Aa) in a sand pill, each named in its tooltip, and the button that unfolds the palette beside it; unfolded, every section a ruled table of 44px cells in a hairline box, a wide key two cells. The tabs' short names are gone (en, ru). Canvas v204.
- **No fog at a strip's end (owner, 2026-11-15: "and such fog at the end of the stripe for scrolling is not good"):** the palette's category strip and its row of values scroll sideways with no fade at either edge; the next item is simply cut by the edge, as Flat's palette is (`flat-score-editor` 01–03).
- **Loop round 28, the quiet key name by Night:** a plain key's name is the key's own ink at 60%, not the page's soft ink, which vanished on a light key in the dark theme; "C3" reads on every keyboard, Day and Night.
- **Loop round 28, one keyboard as the boards draw it (owner, 2026-11-15: "the keyboard is not consistent across the app with new design"):** a plain key's name ("C4") is 12, medium, in soft ink, a lit or marked key's label bold in its own ink, on every keyboard (Practice, the Player, the editor); a white key is never narrower than the phone boards' 26 (about two octaves across a phone), the editor's keys included, which narrowed to 22 to hold both hands (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 28, the editor's bar as its board (owner, 2026-11-15: "but the headers still dont match"):** no band of paper and no hairline; Close a 44 glass circle; Play · Edit one glass pill of 44 segments, 15px, Edit filled in ink; Undo, Redo and ··· together in one glass pill. The Player's title on a phone takes all the room its buttons leave, so "Words: John Newton · Music: New Britain" shows as the board writes it (2026-11-15).
- **The keys hold middle C when the caret has nothing (loop round 18):** with no note at the caret (a new score, an empty bar, a rest with no hand under it), the editor's keys centre on C3 to C5 as the Editor boards draw them, instead of sitting at the bottom of the score's span; with notes at the caret they still follow them.
- **Review your recording ported to its board** (canvas 195): Quantize's five values in one row (Segmented wraps five only past 20 characters, words, never values), and a flush group pads its switch rows 16 itself, so Hide raw notes and Hear notes no longer touch the card's edge.
- **The takes are drawn** (canvas 194, loop; the Takes board): the list (Play in a quiet circle, the name to its page, length and tempo, ··· with Review, Download, Delete at once with Undo), its empty state, and Review your recording as its page with Back in the header: the take as notes, the roll with its split point, Quantize as one row of five (1/4 1/8 1/16 1/4t 1/8t, never 3 + 2), Hide raw notes, Into ›, then Discard and Save to score. The editor's "Takes: 3" opens it.
- **The recording review's Back is in the sheet's header,** as every sheet's page (loop): the "‹ Back" row in its content and `SheetBack` are gone.
- **The recording review's Discard goes at once, with Undo** (R38, loop): the take is back on the Takes page with "Take deleted · Undo"; "Discard this recording?" is gone.
- **A take's Delete goes at once, with Undo** (R38, loop; the board's "Take deleted · Undo brings it back"): from its row's ··· and its page, "Take deleted · Undo" puts it back in its place; the "Delete this take?" dialog is gone. Toasts now sit above every sheet and dialog (z-60), so an Undo said from the Takes sheet is seen and pressed.
- **Loop round 24, R38 in the editor:** deleting an instrument (or hiding the piano) happens at once, "Deleted
  Voice · Undo" in its toast; Undo steps the score back and restores the staves' view; no question, no "Delete…"
  (2026-11-15).
- **The Editor board brought to the ruled tabs (loop, 2026-11-15, canvas 187):** the board still drew Notes ·
  Accidentals · Articulation · Dynamics · Lines · Bar · Text; it now draws the eleven the app holds, each item where
  `EditorPalette.tsx` keeps it (the slur and fingering in Articulations, the pedal in Dynamics, the octave lines in Pitch).
- **One home each, as Flat (2026-11-05):** "we can at the same time edit the time signature from the settings and from
  the palette which is strange". The time signature, key and clef are set in the palette's category, which their
  printed signs open; the title opens Title and credits; the gear holds only what the score does not print.
- **Only what applies (2026-11-05):** "show only the actions relevant at that time like the write as common in the 5/4
  is not relevant and is weird that it is shown but is disabled".
- **The palette as Flat's, its menus too (2026-11-05):** "this menu in the palette doesn't follow how the flat editor
  does this … as close as possible as the flat editor and its ui and its management and features". Every section in
  one scroll; Time signature as Flat's tiles with Customize; Key signature and Clef bare tables; Tempo in Measure.
- **Professional wording (2026-11-05):** "write professionally, not plain words": precise notation terms and concise
  sentences, as Apple, Flat, MuseScore and Dorico write them, in English and Russian.
- **A real notation app:** "proper scores, proper functions like a real music notation software". Like Flat's: "the same
  features and same music notation and same icons and styles". No hand separation, pattern adding or verse adding inside it.
- **Laptop:** a docked side panel and a note-entry toolbar, never a folded bar at the foot ("annoying and not movable").
  **Phone:** a bottom sheet that shrinks the score, usable as Sibelius, with no page overflow. All staves the same width.
- **Palette:** a real card that keeps its height between tabs and never flickers. Folded, one scrollable row; open, the
  sections as structured, animating well. Tools that only repeat a tap on the sheet go. Navigation and delete leave the note
  tools.
- **Menus:** names fit and say what they do. Bar length and clef change are not "Add". Text terms (dolce) read before
  opening. Real terms: crescendo. Each action its own meaningful icon, never one eye for all. Repeat icons fit.
- **Fingering** is an option where it belongs, never a palette mode.
- **Notation we have, as Flat has:** 64ths, tuplets, octave lines, glissando, every articulation and dynamic, repeats ×N,
  endings 3–4, page breaks, bar repeats, multi-bar rests, tempo changes, Roman numerals, free text, two-note tremolo, grace
  notes of both kinds. Shortcuts in every tooltip; the chord wizard.
- **Editing on the score:** everything printed is editable there. No staff selector. A note chosen, then a value tapped,
  changes that note, and the edge between editing and adding is obvious. Delete goes back note by note. Drag a note up and
  down. Chords typed, or chosen with the app's full chord interface.
- **Staves** are plain: add, remove, rename, hide. Hiding never deletes notes; a toggle never clears the score. A song can
  be a single staff.
- **Every song is editable.** Where something cannot be, the learner is told why.
- **Recording:** the learner chooses what plays along and what shows. The pedal prints one mark per press.
- **The editor's draft (canvas 135, 2026-11-11):** "no roles, no edit mode": a built-in song's original is edited by an editor as a draft under a Publishing pill with Publish…, Versions and Discard changes; what reaches every learner asks first.
- **Loop round 61, Tie writes the next note tied (research F5):** with the caret after a written note or chord, `,` holds it on at the value chosen and moves on, as Dorico's Tie in note input; a note after it of the same key is still joined (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, accidentals reach the note just written (research F6):** with nothing chosen, ♮ ♯ ♭ (0 = −) and Respell change the note or chord the caret last wrote, as Flat's and Dorico's do; no new key (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, chords by interval (research F7):** ⌥2 to ⌥8 add a note a 2nd to an octave above the chord chosen or just written, in the key, ⇧⌥ below, as Flat's; listed in the shortcuts sheet (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, a bar on Cmd (research F10):** ⌘← ⌘→ the previous or next bar and ⇧⌘ extend to it, as Flat and Dorico; Home and End keep the score's ends; Alt with ← → does nothing (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, ⌘A chooses the whole staff (research F12):** Cmd or Ctrl with A selects every note of the staff at the caret, as Flat's and Dorico's, so an accidental, a value or Delete reaches all of it; listed in the shortcuts sheet (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the bar keys as Flat's (research F14, F16, F18):** ⌥Enter adds a bar after, ⌘⇧Enter one at the end, ⌘Enter the system break, one key pressed where a line starts there and pressed again to join it ("Join lines" is gone); ⌥. the double dot; the shortcuts sheet says the palette's words (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the palette folds without a jump (research F19):** folding and unfolding cross-fade the tool row and the sections in 150 ms on the app's curve, the sections rising 8; instant under reduced motion (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Repeat (research F13):** a key after Rest and ⇧R write the notes chosen, else the note or chord just written, again right after them, the copy chosen, as Flat's and Dorico's R; the clipboard is untouched. The canvas's palette now carries the app's keys and shortcuts (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Voice 2 (research F8):** a key after Chord in Input mode and ⇧V write the piano staff's second voice, stems down under the first, as Flat's and Dorico's V: a held note under a moving tune; each voice's edits leave the other's notes; greyed on a single staff (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, cross-staff (research F9):** Cross to upper staff and Cross to lower staff after the octave pair in Pitch, ⌘⇧↑ ⌘⇧↓, as Dorico's: the notes drawn on the piano's other staff, still their hand's; the key toward their own staff brings them back (2026-11-17).
- **The tempo's pickers say their name once (loop 22, 2026-11-15):** Beat and Tempo text stand at the row's start, so
  their pickers show the value alone (♩, None), in the editor's Tempo sheet and the New song sheet alike.

## Pickers and tempo

- **Time signature:** Current and Pickup columns, numbers stacked large on staff lines as wheels; a cut or common switch; ✗ ✓
  at the bottom right. Every meter, such as 2/2, 3/2, 5/4, 6/4, 7/4, 3/8, 9/8.
- **Key:** a key-signature grid like the music editors', 1–7 sharps over 1–7 flats, the chosen tinted. **No circle of fifths
  as a picker.** C♯/D♭ written as the reference apps write them.
- **Clef:** a bottom sheet with a grid of every clef and its 8va, 8vb, 15ma, 15mb.
- **Tempo:** a large accent circle with BPM and its word, beat units below; hold and swipe to a full-screen ruler, Largo to
  Presto.
- **One control each,** one key picker, one stepper and menu style.
- **The Player's small buttons reach 44 (2026-11-19, the lines; 44px targets):** on the phone and the laptop alike, the Practice sheet's − and + of the tempo and its Tap stood 40, as did a pattern row's heart and Listen and the Pattern sheet's Sort and filter; each is now 44, the size of every other touch target on the phone.

## Player and editor together
- **The band on the scale (the loop, 2026-11-27; spacing-audit):** the band's pills sat 10 and 14 in with a 6 gap, the transport's circles 10 apart; they are 12 on the glyph's side and 16 on the word's (still optical), 8 between glyph and word, the circles 8 apart.
- **Corners on the scale (the loop, 2026-11-27; laws, the design system as layers, 1):** the rail's chosen tile was 10, the pop-up values in a card 8 and the printed title's Credits target 8, none of DESIGN.md's steps; they are 9, 9 (the 12 card less its 4 inset) and 12.
- **Each field brings its keyboard; Credits a form over the score on a laptop (the loop, 2026-11-26; laws, a phone is not a small laptop, 5; FLAT_SETTINGS_TOOLS_FLOWS §1.3):** Credits' name fields capitalise words and step Next, the licence capitalises sentences, Search for an act is a search field with the Search key and no capitals, Version name ends Done. The laptop editor's printed title had stopped opening Credits (a pattern drifted from the credits' 16 margin and matched nothing in silence; it now fails the build); there Credits is Flat's Edit Score Credits dialog, 640 over the score with Cancel and Save, never a page that hides the editor. One Rename version dialog for both sizes.
- **The Player's rail ends as the editor's (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_UI_SHELL_AND_MOTION §1.2):** Search for an act and Shortcuts at its foot, with the Player's own acts and keys; both cards come from the kit with the one scrim.
- **Shortcuts lists every key the tooltips teach (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_SETTINGS_TOOLS_FLOWS §3.3):** the editor's tooltips taught Go to start, Loop, Delete, Practice, Edit, Piano keys, Transpose, Actual size and Print keys the Shortcuts sheet never listed; it now lists them all once, in Flat's groups (Playback, Notes, Editing, View), and a control shows one key (Zoom in ⌘+, not ⌘= elsewhere).
- **The rail moves with the arrow keys (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_UI_SHELL_AND_MOTION §1.2):** ArrowUp and ArrowDown move between the panels, wrapping, as Flat's rail.
- **One motion for the Player and editor (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_UI_SHELL_AND_MOTION §3):** their boards drew none; now a menu grows from its button (the title's from the left, ☰'s from the right), a popover from the button above it, a page slides in from the right as Flat's PanelStack pushes, the toast rises and the scrim fades, on the one curve; leaving is instant and nothing moves under reduced motion.
- **A disabled tool says why (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_UI_SHELL_AND_MOTION §2.2):** a greyed Undo, Redo, Zoom in or Zoom out, or the last shown staff's eye, ignored the pointer and gave no reason; it now stays hoverable and its tooltip names the reason ("Undo: nothing to undo", "Zoom in: at the largest size"), its icon dimmed to 50%.
- **A rail tooltip only where it says more (the loop, 2026-11-26; FLAT_UI_SHELL_AND_MOTION §1.2):** every rail entry named itself again on hover ("View" over the word View); Flat's rail shows a tooltip only for a label cut short. Ours keeps one only where it adds a shortcut (Search · ⌘K, Shortcuts · ?).
- **The title menu in the ☰'s shape (the loop, 2026-11-26):** the laptop's name menu ran Recordings straight into Share link, Export and Print, where the phone's ☰ sets the file's acts apart after a hairline; both now draw it, Export's submenu still level with its row.
- **No "Song" option; every act its own row (owner, 2026-11-26: "I dont like that teh option is called songs because a song actually is a score with words but it can also be just a musical piece without words like moonlight sonata … and I dont like very much the all these options are now hidden just under this option"; "I meant the option in the player and editor"):** the phone ☰ had one "Song" row hiding the versions, Transpose, Credits, Recordings, Share link, Export and Print. ☰ now lists each in its own row, as Flat's ☰ (flat-score-editor 083): Versions (its value the open version) with its own page titled Versions, then the places (View, Instruments, Layout, MIDI keyboard), Transpose, Credits, Recordings, Version history, and after a hairline Share link, Export, Print; every page's Back returns to ☰. The laptop rail's "Song" entry, which held only the versions, is **Versions** with the layers glyph, its panel titled Versions; the versions list stands bare under the title, no fold head repeating it. (A Library rename of the Songs place, made on a misreading, was reverted.)

The title's menu is the song's general options alone, the same rows in both modes: Recordings, Share link, Export ›, Print, then Duplicate song and Archive song; Credits open from the printed title in Edit. The versions live in the sidebar's Song panel, in both modes (the editor's rail gains Song): each version a row (the open one checked, Default marked, its kind under it), New version, New version with accompaniment (while the open one has none), then Rename…, Set as default and Delete. A version with accompaniment shows the whole-song Accompaniment (Pattern, First chord's inversion, Voicing) under the versions, as before; the editor's Notation panel still writes a pattern into chosen bars. The page prints the open version in both modes. The transport is one glass bar centred on the music: Listen (or Wait) and Loop, then ‹ Play › with Play the honey circle, then the hands and the keys; "Play" no longer names the pace beside the Play button.
- **R74, a song and its versions; the accompaniment written in the editor (owner, 2026-11-26: "now remove the acompaniment and score switch: now we have a song and a song can have multiple versions of itself like just a score or my own version of this score with accompaniment: and when opening a songs now it open the default version. this versions can be edited as the user wants and the title of this versions … and also we should merge the accompaniment paterns in the editor so that the user can add a pattern for this specific measure that he has selected"):** the Accompaniment · Score switch is gone. The version menu beside the name lists the song's versions, each a name and a line (Original: melody, chords and words; My version: with a piano accompaniment), the default marked Default and opened first, the current checked; under a hairline New version (a copy of the current, "My version 2"), Rename… (a dialog: the name, Cancel, Save), Set as default and Delete version in crimson (at once, Undo in its toast). Original prints the melody alone, as a lead sheet; Edit stands beside every version. The Player's Song panel keeps Transpose and Sections: the accompaniment's patterns moved into the editor, where the Notation panel (the phone's open palette) heads with Accompaniment and the selected bars ("Bar 4"): None and the numbered patterns, the chosen checked, a pick writing it into those bars ("Bass + chords written in bar 4 · Undo"), All patterns › to the library, as Band-in-a-Box and Flat apply a style to a range. The editor's draft of a built-in song edits its Original.
- **R74, the song's acts in its name's menu, no Settings window (owner, 2026-11-26: "fix the settings: it doenst make sense with this window popup: make proper controls or popup"):** on the laptop Player the name's menu, "Amazing Grace ▾", holds the acts themselves: Share link, Export › (a submenu beside the menu: MusicXML, Compressed MusicXML (.mxl), MIDI), Print, then after a hairline Duplicate and Archive. Each answers in the toast over the music (Link copied; Archive with Undo). The Settings row and its centred window over the Player are gone from the Player.
- **R74, the views in the View panel (owner, 2026-11-26: "the switch between the view sheet or diagramms … in the right … doesnt make so much sense"):** the Sheet · Diagrams · Grid · Lyrics switch left the bar's right end: it heads the View panel as four cards, the chosen view's settings under them, as Chordify's "Pick your view" (chordify 002) puts the view and its options in one place. Your arrangement and Edit the original are gone (owner: "we dont need this your version"); the version menu is the song's versions (the line above).
- **R74, the laptop Player as the editor: a rail and one panel (owner, 2026-11-26: "i dont like that all that we have in the player is hidden in the settings. like make the player like the editor with its sidebard"; "we added a easier way to switch between the versions … and we stil have this in the settings"; "concentrate on the laptop version"):** the gear and its card are gone. A 76 rail at the left holds Song (the accompaniment's Pattern, First chord's inversion, Voicing; Transpose; Sections) and View (what the Sheet, Diagrams, Grid or Lyrics show), each opening its 352 panel beside the music, Song open when the song opens; the active entry again folds it, the music and the transport moving beside it. Pattern, Transpose and Sort and filter open in the panel's place with Back. The version is the bar's alone: Arrangement and Your arrangement left the panel. The song's acts (Settings, Edit the original, Print, Export, Share link) are the name's menu, "Amazing Grace ▾", as the editor's title. The rail and panel head are the kit's (`_RAILBTN`, `_PHEAD`), one implementation with the editor. The phone waits.
- **The credits on one line in Play and in Edit (loop, 2026-11-26):** the Player prints "Words: John Newton · Music: New Britain (American folk tune)" on one line across the page; the editor's score page sat inside two paddings and broke it after "folk". On a phone the line now spans the page's margins, as the Player's.
- **The Player's Pattern sheet holds the library's shape (the layers walker, 2026-11-17):** its place chips were 34 tall in a row cut at the sheet's edge, and Close all a 28-tall link in an orange at 3.9:1. The chips reach 44 as the kit's and wrap in the sheet (nothing is cut in a sheet); Close all reaches 44 in the darker orange (#A8551F, as Continue's label).
- **The score's systems keep Verovio's spacing (port P4, 2026-11-18):** the board measures 24 between its drawn systems' boxes, chord symbols and bar numbers included; in the app the gap is the engraving's own (`spacingSystem`, the learner's Space between systems on a page), set by ink and not by the 4/8 scale. Nothing is forced to 24: an engraver's spacing outranks a screen's grid (LilyPond, Verovio).
- **The Player's two saved boards brought level (2026-11-18):** PlayerApp and PlayerDesk come from the published copy, not a generator, and still drew the Pattern sheet's chips 34 tall in a cut row, Close all 28 tall at 3.9:1 and each pattern's hands on one cut line; their chips now reach 44 on the kit chip's 4px transparent edges and wrap, Close all reaches 44 in #A8551F, and the hands line takes two lines (lines tool: 0 problems at 402 and 1710). Their favourite hearts were honey-deep where every library board's heart is the red #B84A3E (rest #B4AAA0); they are the library's now (similarity: one heart). Ported (E1): the app's Pattern sheet is the library's shape, one implementation with Accompaniment (`useLibraryArrangement`, `libraryShown`); Texture and Feel filters wait for the patterns to carry that data.
- **The next lyric line readable (loop 63, 2026-11-17, canvas v460):** under the Diagrams view's current line the next one was #A39A90 on the paper, 2.5:1; it is soft ink now (#685E56, 4.5:1 and over), its lighter weight keeping it second (contrast, WCAG 1.4.3). `PlayerApp`, `PlayerDesk`.

- **Loop round 61, a laptop's settings are a card under their button (EditorLaptop's and PlayerDesk's popover):** the editor's gear and the Player's Settings opened as a full-height panel at the right, which covered the editor's Undo, Redo and ···, cut its layout pill, and squeezed the Player's music; both are now the board's card, 400 wide, 16 round with the hairline and the second shade, hung 8 under the bar's buttons at their right edge, fading in place, the music keeping its width, a click outside closing it (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 32, a key down and the chosen note's keys in sky:** the boards' piano paints a key down `#85B8DC` (`--key-down`), the score's selection colour; the app had painted it and the editor's chosen keys honey, the one action's colour. Every keyboard now (2026-11-15). The editor's credits line reaches 44 under the title (it was 38).
- **Loop round 32, the Keyboard button wears one glyph:** the keys, whatever the place shows; its caption says 3D, 2D or Off (it had swapped to a hand or a crossed eye). The Player boards drew a grand piano there and now draw the keys too, the one piano glyph (2026-11-15).
- **One score in two modes:** "I don't like how the header looks with this bar under the header". One designed header, the
  same transport at the foot, Edit straight from the Player, nothing jumping.
- **One gear per screen** (Flat's), Edit beside the title,. Settings never scattered over four menus.
- **Transpose** works exactly the same in both: semitones, the key it lands in, Apply to key or chords, a preview. No circle
  of fifths.
- **Floating controls** hide when the learner taps elsewhere, reliably. Nothing traps a click.

- **One heading on the score in Play and in Edit (loop 63, 2026-11-17):** the editor printed the song's heading its own way (a 22 title, the credits pushed right on the verse's line); it now prints the Player's, value for value: the title centred (24 on a phone, 36 on a laptop), the credits centred under it, then the verse and tempo at the score's left 16 (24) below, the title at the same height in both modes, so switching Play and Edit changes the tools, not the page (consistency, Jakob; the flow lines). Only the laptop editor's docked palette moves the column, as ruled (canvas v421).

## Sheet, selection and playhead

- **Names:** Page view, Vertical scroll, Panorama.
- **Quality:** always sharp at every zoom. The page view looks printed: margins, line breaks, chord sizes. Study real scores,
  LilyPond and abcjs. Verovio, the owner's choice.
- **The page canvas** floats in infinite space, dragged and pinch-zoomed. Vertical scroll takes the full width, every line
  equal.
- **Selection:** tight to the notes, padded from neighbours, the same everywhere; one note of a chord can be chosen; a range
  is exactly the bars chosen; it works from the keyboard. A note clicked in the Player jumps to that note.
- **Two marks:** what is chosen and what is playing are separate; playing is one line across the staves in sight.
- **The editor's selection tight to the note (loop round 58, held against Dorico `001`–`005` and Flat):** the chosen note wore a sky column from over its chord symbol to under the staff, picking the chord and empty staff with it; it now wraps the note's head and stem alone, as Dorico and Flat mark the note itself, on the editor's four boards (2026-11-16).
- **A score's words never touch (loop round 58, engraving):** on a phone the song's page set "zing grace," and "that saved" against each other, the notes spaced by duration alone; each note now advances at least half of its word and the next one's plus a gap, the rest of the line shared by duration, as an engraver widens a bar for its lyrics; wherever the melody is drawn (2026-11-16).
- **A chord after a line's last word stays on the chord row (loop round 58, the Player's Lyrics):** D⁷ after "like me!" and G after "I see." dropped onto the words' line, their empty word giving the column no height; each word now keeps its line's height, so every chord stands over the line, phone and laptop (2026-11-16).
- **Chord names** are not cluttered or too big; a long chord never breaks the layout.

## Player views and settings
- **Played through stands beside the shipped end screens (loop, 2026-11-26, Mobbin):** Quizlet's end of a game (https://mobbin.com/screens/22670919-b826-45b1-94f7-0f29d33a45ef) is two figure cards side by side, the score and the best, then Play again and one primary act; Played through is that shape with the bars between, and with its streak line gone it ends on its acts as Quizlet's does. No change.
- **A laptop view's settings hang under the views (loop, 2026-11-26; "A laptop's settings hang where their bar is"):** Sheet, Diagrams, Grid and Lyrics settings opened at offsets from the window's centre, kept from when the views pill stood there, so the card hung under nothing; it hangs on the bar's 24 edge at the right, 76 down, under the views and the gear.
- **Played through ends on its acts (owner, 2026-11-26: "remove all the thigns that i didnt asked for"):** the line under Play again, "4 min added to today’s practice · 23 days in a row ›", was our own (loop round 61) and said again what the Profile's streak says; it goes, on both sizes.
- **The account menu leads each item with its picture, as Claude's (the owner's saved claude.ai, 2026-11-20):** Profile, Settings, Language, Sign out and Sign in each lead with their Lucide picture (16, ink), 12 before the word; Language keeps its value at the end. `lapwin.py` and `SidebarAccount`.
- **Piano is a popover on a laptop, as PlayerLaptopKeyboard draws it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** over the transport's Keyboard button, 380 wide, 16 round, 12 in, its title "Piano" at its left with no Done; it was the phone's bottom sheet across the laptop's foot. Practice and Piano share one kit layer (`TransportPopover`); on Chords and the Practice pages a laptop's Piano is the card hung at the bar's right, as their boards draw it. A phone keeps the sheet.
- **The loop in honey, named on its button, as PlayerApp and PlayerDesk draw it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** looped bars are a 12% honey band on the sheet (the Grid's, the Lyrics' and the Diagrams' beats the yellow wash), each end a 3 honey rule with a white grip 26 × 52, 8 round, its chevron honey; they wore the editor's selection blue, which is a selection, not the practice. The transport's Loop turns honey while bars are looped and says which: "5–8" under its picture on a phone, "Bars 5–8" on a laptop, "Loop bars 5–8, remove" to a screen reader.
- **Practice is a popover on a laptop, as PlayerDesk draws it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** 420 wide over its transport button, 16 round, 12 in, its title "Practice" in the sans at its left with no Done; it was the phone's bottom sheet across the laptop's foot. A phone keeps the sheet.

The song Player had none of them (only a scale's exercise had its rest); the loop played on until stopped.
- **The Practice sheet's tempo as PlayerApp draws it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** the meter (13 bold, soft) with a dot a beat, the first 16 and the rest 12, the one sounding lit in honey while the music plays; − and + 44 round a step of 1 either side of the BPM (40 bold, "BPM · Andante" 12 under it), then a Tap pill; "Score tempo: 80 BPM" a 30 pill under them once the tempo is changed. It was the Metronome's −10 −1 +1 +10 face with Tap tempo and Score tempo as buttons. Metronome is one row, its Off · Count-in · On 236 wide at its end, and a song starts on Count-in, as the board. The board's Metronome sound row and page went: the Player's click does not follow the Metronome's sound, whose one home is the Metronome, and its sounds (Wood block, Claves) were none of the app's; the board now draws "Connect a piano or show the Keyboard." under Wait when no piano is there, as the app.

- **Loop round 25, the Settings' head as the board's (PlayerApp):** the song's tile with its two notes, its title 17 bold over "G major · 3/4 · 80 BPM" in 13, Share link and ··· white circles lifted a little, Edit score 44 tall with 12 corners in 15 semibold (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, the transport's groups as the board measures them (PlayerApp, PlayerDesk):** three glass groups 56 tall, 4 inside, 8 apart, every button 48 tall (‹ › 44 wide, Play 48) on a phone from 390px; on a laptop each picture stands beside its word in 15px semibold, 12 before and 16 after (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, the transport floats over the music (the board's PlayerApp):** its three glass groups stand over the music's foot with no band or hairline under them, the music running on beneath to the screen's edge and scrolling clear of the keys' window, else of the transport; the R31-P row at the foot is gone (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the title in the bar, no floating circles (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "the titles doesnt look good and should be in the headers ... no settings in the view buttons. floating buttons with backroung doesnt make sense"; canvas v201):** the Player's bar is a band of paper with its hairline: Close and the gear plain icons, the song's title (the page's heading) and who wrote it beside Close; the views a pill in the middle from 640px, on a phone one button wearing the view shown that opens the four as a menu. A view button only shows its view. No view prints the title any more (lines, across, pages, Grid, Diagrams, Lyrics); a printed score keeps its own (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the loop's grips drag across lines (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "the drag holders to loop doesnt work"):** a grip was held to its own line, and a phone lays one or two bars to a line, so it could not move; now each line marks its bars, a grip follows the finger over the whole score (the window keeps the drag when the grip moves to another line), the start from the first bar to the loop's end and the end from its start to the piece's last bar, and the click a drag ends with is swallowed. The score's scroller keeps 12px each side so the end grip's tab is never cut (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the piano a window over the music (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "piano should also be floating and draggabble and resizeble from all the angles"; canvas v202):** the keys, 3D or 2D, are a rounded, lifted window over the music's foot above the transport (the phone's 8px from each side, the laptop's 1100 wide and centred, as the board draws them), never a docked row. Its grip along the top moves it (the arrow keys too; a double tap puts it back); a handle at each corner and on the left, right and bottom sizes it. Each screen size keeps its own window; the music scrolls clear of it and the first tip sits above it. The keys' dock setting no longer acts in the Player; the divider is gone (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the Parts sheet (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "hands settings should be refactored"; the board's Parts, R43 canvas 119):** the transport's hands open Parts: a card for each hand, the right first, with its picture in its paint, its name and a switch for whether you play it (one stays on), its volume (how loud the app plays it when you don't; 0, not at all) and, on the hand you don't play, Dim. Left · Both · Right, Hear the other hand, Player settings › Hands and the other hand's Hide are gone: the volume and Dim say it all, in one home (2026-11-15).
- **No pedal under silence (loop round 18):** music with no chord symbols and no pedal marks is pedalled bar by bar only where a bar sounds a note; a new song's empty bars, or a bar of rests, carry no Ped. and no pedal is held.

  mix still says "Left hand changed"); the Pattern list shows each pattern's two hands as bars, as the hands' lists
  do, with a line only for why one cannot be chosen ("Needs a melody staff"); what a pattern is stays in Pattern's ⓘ
  (2026-11-15).

Every segmented control is the board's SEG: each segment as wide as its words, never wrapped, the room left shared (2026-11-15).

- **A sheet's height is checked, not assumed; a tiny target the tap ignores becomes the real target (R43, canvas
  122):** "the piano settings in the player are not correct height and the rows don't have enough place". Rows shrank
  in a flex sheet and the sheet ran under the bar. Every sheet's children are `flex: none`; a phone sheet stops below
  the bar. Played through's 18-wide bars practised four at a time anyway, so the four became the button. The Chord
  player got the one Piano sheet and the same live 3D code, and the Player got Night through the Chords page's map.
- **One thing, one markup, imported by every page that has it (R43, canvas 121):** "merge them and make proper one
  piano settings". The Player's and the Chords page's piano sheets had drifted (Angle on one, Hands on the keys and
  the Range strip on the other). Now `piano_sheet.py` draws the one Piano sheet for both; only the choices follow
  the page (a chord's octaves, a song's own piano).
- **Measure before saying a turn is done (R43, canvas 120):** "did you check the paddings, the spacing and all other?".
  A screenshot is not a check. Each publish runs `spacing_measure.js`, `layout_check.js` and the hit-area count on every
  board the turn touched, and the report quotes the numbers.
- **A menu is named by what it holds, from the references, not the owner's draft word (R43, canvas 119):** "research
  and what professional terms should we use and dont take my advice as such". Hands became **Parts** (Yamaha,
  Synthesia, Flat). The rows are Right hand and Left hand, not "Piano left hand", and the hand's picture is the same in
  the bar and the menu. Honey chips for on/off became switch rows.
- **One icon, one meaning, defined once (R43, canvas 118):** "why the icons of the play mode differ in the drawer and
  in the toolbar". A mode's picture is one map read by every place that shows the mode; Play is a metronome, Wait an
  hourglass, everywhere.
- **A style carried over means every sheet and control, not the bar alone (R43, canvas 117):** "but you didnt do the
  proper refactoring of the settings of the buttons of the styles and so on". The Player's sheets now use the Chords
  kit throughout: centred title and Done, green switches, plain groups, radio cards with a check, Keyboard as Chords'.
- **The Player speaks the Chords page's language (R43, canvas 116):** "update the player with the same style updates
  and instructions". The transport as three glass groups, ‹ ▶ › the tinted one, each symbol over its
  value on a phone; Transpose the Chords page's Key (letters, ♭ ♮ ♯ on the chosen letter, spelled by direction, Back
  to the written key). A pattern settled on one page is carried to every page that has the same thing.
- **The end of a run says what happened and what next** (2026-11-07, the redesign skill's evidence of care): Played
  through, the score with its comparison, each bar's slips, Practise bars n–m, Play again, the next step. Drawn (v149).
  At the end of the song only, not after a loop ("song only").
- **No Song information and no Song page row in the Player** ("no song information", "no song page in the settings"). Built in loop round 1 (2026-11-13), with My recordings, How did I play, Voice commands, the Rhythm game and
  the Tuner gone from the Player; the recording's Tuning is a chip on the song page's fact line.
- **A settings drawer holds settings; its acts sit in its head** ("the first two three settings should be more
  distinctive", "feels like a bottom bar and not like settings drawer", "still not good. research more"): the song at
  the drawer's head with Share link and ··· (Print · Export), as Chordify's ⋮; settings under headers below.
  Never a row of icon buttons in a drawer, never acts listed among settings.
  Edit score takes the whole row under the song's name, Share link and ··· beside the name, never Edit inside ···
  ("the edit button is too hidden", "the edit button takes the whole row"); no section
  headings over the drawer's groups ("i don't like the headings or titles of the settings sections").
- **Settings a learner understands, ordered by what matters to them;** the sheet's View settings usable; nothing reserves
  a band above the keyboard, nor a tip; proper pedals, not a lever at the keys' corner (piano sites as the reference);
  the Player smooth with many notes (the Fantaisie hung). Build the approved mockup whole, "not only half" (2026-11-05).
  `/settings/player` gone, the gear the Player's settings' home.
- **Proper pianos, proper pedals (2026-11-05):** "i need proper pianos with proper pedal and implementations" (musicca,
  piano.org). Built in R33: ivory and ebony keys with felt and depth everywhere; the sustain held, latched (a lit
  indicator) or the score's, every key under it released together with a real damper; soft quieter and darker,
  sostenuto as a piano's; the Player's
  piano settings stay in its sheet. Record and Mark not repeated on the keys (the editor's Record, the Chord finder).
- **Songscription's keyboard (2026-11-05):** "it doesn't still look good with these, and this rail at the bottom, look
  at the songscription piano implementation"; "i don't want finger number under the keyboard and no piano roll yet".
  Built in R34: the rail gone, the keys slim and edge to edge, the chord held; no piano roll.

- **The Player's Pace and metronome (2026-11-07, canvas R37):**
  - Modes are Wait and Play only, no Follow, no group titles.
  - Sheet settings have no Your marks switch: the learner's own marks always show, and are removed by deleting them.
  - The transport's menu is Practice, never Pace. It holds Wait · Play, the metronome, and the loop. A song's recording has no settings anywhere in the Player: it
    plays along, and a wrong start is fixed in the content.
  - The Song gear has two radio cards: Arrangement (its Level) and Accompaniment
    (Pattern ›). Versions and accompaniment are separate, one plays. Rejected: "As written", a switch over version
    tiles, one mixed list. Names are the trade's: flowkey's levels, Smart Pianist's Accompaniment and Pattern. No Voicing or Swing; the accompaniment is not in
    Practice.
  - The Player's Pattern page is a library: Search; place chips at the top (All, Favourites, Recently used, each
    book, Mine; the owner likes them); a Sort and filter page (Sort by, Level, Texture, Feel,
    Reset · Show n) with its count on the button and removable chips; folders that fold by their header (Recently
    used and Favourites open, each book closed, its groups inside, Mine's own folders), Close all / Open all; rows with roll, hands line, level, Listen, heart. Never chips by
    kind, stars or cards two a row.
  - The Player keeps Sections, Print and Share link. No My recordings, no How did I play, no Voice commands, anywhere; no Tuner in the Player.
  - The metronome is Flat's: one Off · Count-in · On choice under the tempo, and Metronome sound › opens a page
    (sound list with a check, Volume, Back). A choice with its own list opens a page, as Flat's Playback options do.
  - No cursor or scrolling settings at all, neither a switch nor choices (rejected twice). The cursor moves by
    note and the score scrolls when needed; a scroll by hand shows a Back to the music pill.
  - No ask-on-open, no skin tone (one neutral hand), no resume-following setting.
  - The tempo belongs to the metronome, in BPM with − + Tap and its marking, Score tempo to go back.
  - One setting never disables another.
  - The meter and accents come from the score: no beat squares, no subdivision in the Player.
  - Sounds named for what they are, and every one plays.
  - References are sheet-music players, never recording software.
  - No pedal setting: "remove it". Playback pedals as marked or automatically.
  - The sheet's Show chips are the app's names. No cursor setting.
  - The full list is in `.scratch/r37/plan/pace-and-settings.md`, "The rulings as they stand".
- Every Chordify feature but guitar. Plain which view a setting belongs to; Layout is the sheet music's alone.
- **Diagrams** animate; strip and diagrams swipe by themselves, the page never moves; no odd scroll bar; a swipe goes to the
  next chord.
- **Taps on chords** move the player there and open no dialog.
- **Diagrams as its board draws it (owner, 2026-11-16: "why did you wrongly refactored and implemented the flow and the functions of the diagram view of the player"; loop round 61):** the app had kept its older pair of two fixed slots and a strip that turned pages, and its unframed diagram, instead of porting the board's code. Now: a row of every change, the one sounding at the start and the rest at 38%, swiped a chord at a time (the music follows) or tapped; the beat strip one row of 36px cells (52 on a laptop) following the beat a third of the way in; the line sung under it; the title's credits under the title; one framed chord tile app-wide.
- **Settings:** a song with written staves plays its staves; patterns, inversion and voice leading belong to a chord chart
  alone; "Melody" goes. A section can have its own pattern. Old settings nobody needs go.
- **Drum grooves:** Chordify's.
- **The Practice sheet's tempo and metronome are one group (loop 22, 2026-11-15):** under Wait · Play, one white card,
  the tempo's steps with BPM over them, then Metronome Off · Count-in · On, a hairline
  between, as Scale settings' rows; the steps tighten to fit a 393 phone.
- **Player settings › Playback keeps its gap (loop 22, 2026-11-15):** the metronome's card and Speed training's stood
  touching; the page is a column with the sheet's 16 between its cards.

- **Loop round 61, Wait mode waits for keys:** the keys as written, not their names; the right name in another octave says "Play it an octave higher" or "lower" (Chordio, Metronaut; 2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 61, a Wait pass's score and best (research items 2, 4):** a pass ends "Played through · Score 40%" with New best or Best n% beside it, one hardest-bars rule with the takes'; the line under the transport lifts the keys' window by its height, where it had slid the transport over the keys (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 61, a lesson done from a good pass (research item 15):** a Wait pass counts a lesson done only at 80 % or more, a loop's lap scored on its own bars; a pass with 30 slips no longer ticks it (2026-11-16).

- **The views' headings on the scale (the lines sweep, 2026-11-18):** Lyrics, Grid and Diagrams set their title 6 above the credits and the Grid's verses 12 and 24 apart from mixed margins; the title and credits stand 8 apart and the first verse 12 under the beat strip, each later verse 24, so the lines show no gap off the 4/8 scale on any view (spacing scale; proximity).
- **Diagrams' beat cells reach 44 (loop 63, 2026-11-17):** on a phone the chord grid under the diagrams drew each beat 36 (40 without the keys), under a finger's 44; every beat is now 44 square, with or without the keys, and the heading over the diagrams keeps 8 between the title and the credits, as on the score (Fitts; one heading; canvas v428).
- **The score's systems on the spacing scale (loop 63, 2026-11-17):** the title block sits 16 above the first system on a phone and 24 on a laptop (it read 19 and 39, nearer the score's top than its own group), and the systems stand 24 apart on a phone, 32 on a laptop (22 and 30, off the 4/8 scale); the lines show no gap off the scale (canvas v420).
- **Played through on the cards' edge (loop 63, 2026-11-17):** the sheet's title stood 8 in from the cards under it and Done 4 in from their right, a near-miss; both sit on the cards' edges now, the Bars label too. The two result cards stand 12 apart, as far as their words stand in (they were 8, reading as one); Play again and Try it in Play are 48 as Practise bars is (one size for alike buttons). The laptop's Played through gets its own board beside the phone's (edge-lines: alignment, proximity, similarity; canvas v431).

- **The Pattern sheet's rows say both hands; the song menu is the kit's menu (2026-11-17, the layers sweep):** a pattern's line ("LH: Octave, whole note · RH: Chord on every beat") was cut at "RH: B…"; it now takes two lines before an ellipsis (titles and sublines wrap to two lines), each hand's label bound to its first word. The ··· beside the song in the Player's settings opened a menu with no name and its own corners and shadow; it is now the kit's menu (6 inset, 16 corners, 44 rows on 10 corners, the shared shadow), named Song, as the View menu beside it.

- **One kit part per choice (2026-11-20, "fix all, and use best practices and skills"):** radio tiles drawn by hand on New song (staves, key, time) and the block style editor's presets are now the kit's `ChoiceTiles`, on the same radio group as `Segmented` (the arrows move and choose; a tile under the pointer takes the muted fill), and the style editor's layouts the kit's `PictureTiles`; a song page's list properties (level, genre, language) choose from the kit's `Listbox`, as every picker does, in place of their own radio rows. The look is unchanged.

- **One laptop popover (2026-11-20, "fix all"):** the Player's popovers stood 12 in while the canvas's `POPOVER` and Played through stood 16; every laptop popover is now one shape, 16 round, 16 in, 12 between parts, its title 17 in the sans: on the canvas, and in the app as the kit's `PanelPopover` (Practice, Piano, Go to bar, MIDI keyboard, a song's properties), where four screens had built their own.

- **One named field (2026-11-20, "fix all"):** an exercise's Octaves, Figure and Tonality and a take's Quantize wore their names in grey 14 at the edge, beside Pace and Hands in the kit's soft caps 16 in; `NamedSegmented` is gone and every named choice is the kit's `Labelled` (the canvas's LABELLED). HandsSegmented lost two layout flags nothing used.

- **A laptop's settings hang where their bar is (2026-11-20, "fix all"):** Scale settings was the phone's bottom sheet over a dimmed page on a laptop; it is the card hung under the gear, as ScalesLaptop draws it. Every hung card is the boards' POPOVER, 380 wide: under a page's bar 64 down and 40 in (it was 400 at 76), under the Player's and the editor's shared bar 76 down on the bar's 24 edge (PlayerDesk's Settings and its pages stood 16 in and 68 down, 420 wide, the editor's 24 and 76, 400 and 420; one place and one width now, so Play to Edit moves nothing). The canvas's Piano cards on Scales, Intervals, Progressions and Exercises dropped 70 down, under their settings' 64; now 64. `Sheet`'s `hung` names the bar: `header` or `score-bar`.

- **Each tool's settings in place, as its board (2026-11-20, "fix all"):** Intervals, a progression and the Chord finder sent their gear to a page of Settings (`/settings/intervals`, `/progressions`, `/chord-finder`); their boards open Interval settings, Progression settings and Chord finder settings in place, a sheet on a phone and the card hung under the gear on a laptop. One `SettingsGear` draws the gear and its sheet, named by the sheet's title; the three pages and their routes are gone. The Chord finder's card is its board's one group (Slash chords for inversions, Keys that contain the chord, Key’s scale on the keyboard, Remember the chords played, Forget the chords played, dimmed while nothing is kept) and MIDI devices ›; Forget left the page. The link to the piano's page is named as the page, MIDI devices, on the canvas (Chord finder, Chord player) and in the app (it read Connect a piano there).

- **The code review's round over the kit and the research (2026-11-20, "run the /code-review in loop for the whole app … check the new researches"):** the layers move as the claude.ai research measures them: dialogs grow from 0.98 and popovers, menus and the select from 0.97 over 200ms on the app's one curve, closing in 150, the tooltip in 120 from 0.98, none of them under reduced motion (they opened in 100ms from 0.95); menus stand 6 from their button (the account menu kept 8); a toast stays 6 seconds (5), paused while the pointer is on it. Forget the chords played happens at once with Undo in its toast. Every Practice gear is one `GearButton` saying whether its settings are open (Scales built its own); a sheet's parts stand 16 apart on a phone and 12 in the laptop card, as BSHEET and POPOVER (they were 4, each page wrapping its own 16). Two casts and an unused export went; Scales' staff page took in the one-user screen it wrapped.

- **One bar for how much, on Base UI's Meter (2026-11-20, "check what libraries and components that already exist we can use"):** Publish's Findable, a song's practice shares and the Continue card each drew their own bar with a hand-set `role`; all three are the kit's `ProgressBar` now, built on Base UI's Meter, named and voiced where it stands alone, its paint the row's (learned, honey, ink, soft), and it no longer animates its width. What the researched apps take from libraries, and what we keep, is in `.scratch/r39/plan/libraries-from-research.md`.

- **Choices on the kit's radio group, captions in the sans (2026-11-20, the code review over the kit):** a sheet's one-of-several list (Subdivision, Sound, Publish's) and the score's Layout set `role="radio"` by hand, every row its own tab stop or the arrows rebuilt by hand; both are Base UI's radio group now, one tab stop, the arrows moving and choosing. The octave grid keeps its own arrows, which move in two directions over its four columns. A menu section's caption (Score view's "Sheet music", and 23 others) was set in the serif every h2 takes; it is the sans, as the boards' captions.

- **A toolbar behaves as one (2026-11-20, the code review over the kit):** eleven bars said `role="toolbar"` (the floating bars, the Player's transport, a run's, the editor's selection, annotating, the Chord player's strip, the library's and Your lessons' selection bars, a lesson's toolbar and its example's tools) while every button in them was a Tab stop of its own and the arrows did nothing; only the editor's palette kept the toolbar's promise. All are the kit's `Toolbar` now, on the palette's one roving focus: one Tab stop, ← → along the buttons (↑ ↓ on an upright bar), Home and End, a radio group or field inside keeping its own stop and arrows.

- **Reduced motion is instant everywhere (2026-11-20, the code review against craft-details):** "under reduced motion every transition is instant" held only where a part said `motion-reduce:`; most of the kit's transitions still moved. `theme.css`'s base layer now sets every transition and animation to 0.01ms under `prefers-reduced-motion` (not 0, so Base UI's layers still hear their end and close); a sheet opens and closes at once.

- **One check, one clock, no casts, lint clean (2026-11-20, the code review over the whole app):** a chosen thing wears one `CheckMark` (24 round, a 2 ring, ink while chosen), as the boards' CHECKBOX: Select's, RowCheck's 28 with a 1 ring, a list sheet's square and Your lessons' were four copies. "Today", "3 days ago", "Synced 2 min ago" and a streak's today read one minute clock (`useNow`), outside render and again as the app comes back into view; nine screens called `Date.now()` while rendering and the account card and the calendar kept timers of their own. Effects that set state the render could work out (Played through opening, the best before, the Wait clock's silence, Play all's sounding chord) are worked out in render; no ref is read while rendering; Settings' dialog panes are made once. Every type cast in `src` is a guard or a typed finder (about 300); a page's panel is read one at a time. The dependencies are at their latest in range (tiptap 3.35, verovio 6.4.1, lucide 1.77, motion 14.14, zustand 5.1, supabase-js 2.119); `eslint src` reports no error. Twelve kit parts nothing used went with the tokens only they read (CardGrid, ChordHeading, Fact, FactCard, FavouriteMark, HandCard, RatingMark, ToggleTile, TreeNav, Wheel, MetronomeIcon, PickerRow). Fourteen files and about thirty declarations nothing read went the same way; one of them was a gap, not dead code: a take kept in the editor never reached the Profile's best accuracy, and now does. Helpers written twice or more now have one home each: text in both languages (`isLocalText`, `isWrittenText`, eight copies), a saved count (`isCount`), a line of numerals (`readsAsNumerals`), `isRecord`, `lowestFirst` (four), `eighthTicks`, a voicing's middle, a note's written name, a chord printed with its numbers raised (`RaisedSymbol`, three), and a port's status error (`statusError`, twelve).

## Song page
- **R74, the song page gone, one capsule for version and mode (owner, 2026-11-26: "remove the song overview page"; "make it more visible … that its an accompaniment … somehow maybe we can merge this two"):** a song opens in the Player; its Settings open as a window over it, Edit the original sits in its menu; the bar's capsule names the version (Accompaniment ▾, the Arrangement "None written for this song yet", Your arrangement) and ends in Edit, Done in the editor; the score prints "Accompaniment from the chords" under its credits.
- **A song's name heads its facts (loop, the lines, 2026-11-26):** with the credits gone from under it, the name stood 16 from the cover above and 16 from Key, Time and Tempo below, so it read as the cover's end; it stands 8 over its facts now, the cover 16 above.
- **A song's bar map says only where it runs (owner, 2026-11-26: "remove all the thigns that i didnt asked for"):** "Played 6 times · 42 minutes" between Bar 1 and Bar 16 was our own (loop round 13); how much was practised is the Profile's, so the map's line reads Bar 1 … Bar 16.
- **A section's head says its bars alone (owner, 2026-11-26: "remove all the thigns that i didnt asked for"):** "Learn the Verse" names the section and the Chords list names its chords, so the head's "Verse · bars 1–16" over "G · G7 · C · D · D7 · Em" is now "Bars 1–16".
- **A song's page measured against its boards (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** the title's pencil opened the editor, which the ··· already opens as Edit score (two ways to one act); the Song board's pencil opens Title and credits, so it now opens the editor on that page of its gear (`/edit/$id?credits`, forgotten once open), its glyph 18 as the board's (was 20). ## Learn and the editors
- **One count for a lesson's place (loop, 2026-11-26):** Continue and Search said "Lesson 7 of 13" while the lesson itself says "Lesson 7 of 11": Foundations holds 11 lessons and 2 checks, and Continue counted all 13 steps as lessons. Both read Lesson 7 of 11 now; the level's ring and bar still count its 13 steps.
- **One laptop sub-page bar, measured (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** a large title after Back (Your lessons, Courses, a level, a course) kept the main's 16 above its bar on a laptop, so its Back stood at 22 where every sub-page board's stands at 6, Back 4 further in and the title 8 after it where the kit's LTITLEBAR draws 40 and 12; a lesson's bar stood 8 low. All now the one 56 bar flush at the top, content 24 under it. The Courses and level boards still drew the old 88 head with Back 44 in (a drifted LBACK); they now call LTITLEBAR. Your lessons' sort was a boxed ⇅ Recent ⇕ behind a divider where its board draws the word in ink by a chevron; it is `SortMenu`'s field face now (`SortChoice` gone), and its table head reads on its own line height. Learn is marked in the sidebar on Your lessons and a level's page, as on Courses and a lesson.
- **A lesson's links and question as the boards (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** a link stood in a card with the small solid tile; the board draws it on the paper, a 60 row from the column's edge with its 44 tile on its paint's wash (Layers on sky, List checks on lilac). Show the answer was the ringed white pill; it is the board's pill on the sand wash. The answer's keys named F♯ as "F#"; they print the musician's sharp.
- **A lesson's line of notes and its Note as the boards (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** a line of notes put its Note names toggle (C E) and Play under the staff, where the board heads it with its notes by name ("C D E F G A B C", 17 bold on a 44 row) and Play at the row's end, the staff under it on one row; the toggle is gone (the head names the notes). A Note was a plain sand box at 17; it is the board's aside: sized to its words, "Note" in the soft caps over them, 16 on 1.45, 12 corners, 12 by 16 inside.
- **A lesson measured against its board (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** what it teaches was held to 65ch (736) in a 760 column, so on a laptop it broke to two lines the board sets on one; a scale stood 12 under its words where the board's figures stand 20; an example's keys were 5 short of the board's (the keys and their 5 of felt: 101 on a phone, 155 on a laptop); a section's heading 28 tall where the board's is 27 (its own line height), in the editor too. Each scale is its name, Play and its own keys, named key by key, the tonic in its own colour as the Scales page paints a scale; an interval, a quality on twelve roots and voicings get their own keys too; the question stands open on its keys with "Choose on the keys", "n of 7 notes", Again and Show the answer. The pinned keyboard and the bar's Piano button are gone from a lesson.
- **The Lesson boards read the real lesson (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-20):** the boards drew an invented two-section "How major scales work" (One pattern for every key, Building D major, an In short, an After line, version chips) the app has never had; they now draw `major-scales.ts` block for block: five sections, each scale (C, G, F) named on its own keys, the degrees' line of notes and the fingering's grand staff in Bravura's heads, a Note, the D major question (seven notes in any octave), its two links on their tiles (Layers on sky, List checks on lilac). The foot's "Section n of 5" counts them. The app is ported to these boards next.

- **Lessons, courses and levels, and Learn's structure (2026-11-18, "what is the difference between them … how can we improve this learn page structure. research more and check the examples"; canvas v547):** a lesson is one page of sections learned in one sitting; a level is the main path, its Journey of lessons in order; a course is a short focused topic (reading, rhythm, the pedal) taken beside the path, with its own progress. The examples share one order, and Learn keeps it: Continue first (flowkey's Continue Learning), then the path (Duolingo's sections, the journey app's "0/20 complete"), then the courses on a shelf (flowkey's Courses). What changed: every course card's eyebrow repeated its level in capitals ("BEGINNER" on four of five, which tells the learner nothing); it now says what they hunt for, the card's progress ("3 of 8 lessons", "8 lessons", "Done"), in sentence case (data drives the form; scan-first 7). Examples read: `piano-data/reference-apps/flowkey` 001–003, `duolingo-music` 001, `courses-app/001-home`, `journey-app/001-beginner-journey-foundations`.
